We are grateful to the anonymous reviewers for their insightful comments.
This work was supported by JST ACT-X Grant Number JPMJAX2104; JSPS Overseas Research Fellowships; and JSPS KAKENHI
Grant Numbers JP20H04162, JP20H05703, JP22H03564, and JP24H00699.

Appendix A
Details of HFL

Our
\lambda_{\mathbf{HFL}}
is designed based on
(
Kura, 2023
)
.

Let
\mathbf{Base}
be a set of base types and
\mathbf{BO}
be a set of typed signatures of basic operations.

A.1.
Typing Rules

\frac{(x:\sigma)\in\Gamma}{\Gamma\vdash x:\sigma}\quad\textsc{S-Var}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:\mathrm{ar}(\mathbf{op})}{\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{op}\ M:\mathrm{car}(\mathbf{op})}\quad\textsc{S-BasicOp}\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash():1}\quad\textsc{S-Unit}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma\quad\Gamma\vdash N:\tau}{\Gamma\vdash(M,N):\sigma\times\tau}\quad\textsc{S-Pair}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}}{\Gamma\vdash\pi_{1}\ M:\sigma_{1}}\quad\textsc{S-Fst}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}}{\Gamma\vdash\pi_{2}\ M:\sigma_{2}}\quad\textsc{S-Snd}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:0}{\Gamma\vdash\delta(M):\tau}\quad\textsc{S-Case0}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma_{i}}{\Gamma\vdash\iota_{i}\ M:\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}\quad\textsc{S-Inj}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\quad\Gamma,x_{1}:\sigma_{1}\vdash M_{1}:\tau\quad\Gamma,x_{2}:\sigma_{2}\vdash M_{2}:\tau}{\Gamma\vdash\delta(M,x_{1}:\sigma_{1}.M_{1},x_{2}:\sigma_{2}.M_{2}):\tau}\quad\textsc{S-Case2}\frac{\Gamma,x:\sigma\vdash M:\mathbf{Prop}}{\Gamma\vdash\lambda x:\sigma.M:\sigma\to\mathbf{Prop}}\quad\textsc{S-Abs}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma\to\mathbf{Prop}\quad\Gamma\vdash N:\sigma}{\Gamma\vdash M\ N:\mathbf{Prop}}\quad\textsc{S-App}\frac{\Gamma,f:\sigma\to\mathbf{Prop},\vdash M:\sigma\to\mathbf{Prop}}{\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{fix}\ f.M:\sigma\to\mathbf{Prop}}\quad\textsc{S-Fix}

A.2.
Semantics

Let
\mathbb{C}
be an
\omega\mathbf{CPO}
-enriched cartesian closed category.
The underlying (
\mathbf{Set}
-enriched) category of
\mathbb{C}
is denoted by
\mathbb{C}_{0}
.

Definition A.1.

An object
A\in\mathbb{C}
has a
bottom element
if there exists
\bot^{A}:1\to A
such that for any
f:1\to A
, we have
\bot^{A}\leq f
with respect to the partial order on
\mathbb{C}(1,A)
.

Definition A.2.

We say
\mathbb{C}

admits a lifting strong monad
if

•

the algebraic theory defined by one constant (nullary operation)
\bot
and one inequation
\bot\leq x
has free algebras, that is, the following universal property holds: for any
X\in\mathbb{C}
, there exists
X_{\bot}\in\mathbb{C}
and
\eta_{X}:X\to X_{\bot}
such that if
f:X\to A
is such that
A\in\mathbb{C}
has a bottom element, then there exists a unique
\overline{f}:X_{\bot}\to A
such that
\overline{f}\mathrel{\circ}\eta_{X}=f
, and

•

the induced lifting monad
({-})_{\bot}
is strong.

Note that an Eilenberg–Moore algebra of the lifting monad
({-})_{\bot}
is just an object
A\in\mathbb{C}
with a bottom element.
Note also that a lifting monad defines a uniform
({-})_{\bot}
-fixed-point operator
({-})^{\dagger}:\mathbb{C}_{0}(X_{\bot},X_{\bot})\to\mathbb{C}_{0}(1,X_{\bot})
for any
X\in\mathbb{C}
.

f^{\dagger}:1\to X_{\bot}\quad\coloneqq\quad\sup_{n}f^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot^{X_{\bot}}\qquad\text{for any $f:X_{\bot}\to X_{\bot}$}

It is known that a uniform
T
-fixed-point operator uniquely extends to a parameterised uniform
T
-fixed-point operator
({-})^{\dagger}:\mathbb{C}_{0}(X\times A,A)\to\mathbb{C}_{0}(X,A)
for any Eilenberg–Moore
T
-algebra
\zeta:TA\to A
.

Definition A.3 (
\lambda_{\mathrm{HFL}}
-model).

A
\lambda_{\mathrm{HFL}}
-model
is a tuple
\mathcal{A}=(\mathbb{C},A,a,\Omega)
where

•

\mathbb{C}
is an
\omega\mathbf{CPO}
-enriched bicartesian closed category that admits a lifting monad.

•

A:\mathbf{Base}\to\mathbb{C}_{0}
is an interpretation of each base type.

•

For each
\mathbf{op}:\mathrm{ar}(\mathbf{op})\rightarrowtriangle\mathrm{car}(\mathbf{op})
,
a(\mathbf{op}):\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathrm{ar}(\mathbf{op})\rrbracket\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathrm{car}(\mathbf{op})\rrbracket
is an interpretation of
\mathbf{op}
.
Here,
\mathcal{A}\llbracket-\rrbracket
is the interpretation of types defined in Definition
A.4
.

•

\Omega\in\mathbb{C}
has a bottom element
\bot^{\Omega}:1\to\Omega
.

Note that the definition above is simplified compared to
(
Kura, 2023
)
: we don’t require a pseudo-lifting strong monad
T
on
\mathbb{C}
and an EM
T
-algebra on
\Omega
.
This is because we unified effect-free constants and modal operators as basic operators.
If we have a strong monad from
({-})_{\bot}
to
T
, then an EM
T
-algebra on
\Omega
induces an EM
({-})_{\bot}
-algebra on
\Omega
, and both the uniform
T
-fixed-point operator and the uniform
({-})_{\bot}
-fixed-point operator give the same interpretation of fixed points.

Definition A.4 (interpretation of types).

For each type
\sigma
, its interpretation
\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket
is defined as follows.

(41)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\llbracket b\rrbracket\coloneqq Ab\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathbf{Prop}\rrbracket\coloneqq\Omega\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket 0\rrbracket\coloneqq 0\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma+\tau\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket+\mathcal{A}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket

(42)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\llbracket 1\rrbracket\coloneqq 1\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\times\tau\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket\times\mathcal{A}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\to\mathbf{Prop}\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket\Rightarrow\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathbf{Prop}\rrbracket

Definition A.5 (interpretation of contexts).

The interpretation
\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
of a context is defined as follows.

\mathcal{A}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket\coloneqq 1\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma,x:\sigma\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\times\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket

Definition A.6 (interpretation of terms).

For any well-typed term
\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma
, its interpretation
\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket=\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma\rrbracket:\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket
is defined as follows.

(43)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma,x:\sigma\vdash y:\tau\rrbracket\coloneqq\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash y:\tau\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}&x\neq y\\
\pi_{2}&x=y\end{cases}

(44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathbf{op}\ M\rrbracket\coloneqq a(\mathbf{op})\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket()\rrbracket\coloneqq{!}\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket(M_{1},M_{2})\rrbracket\coloneqq\langle\mathcal{A}\llbracket M_{1}\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket M_{2}\rrbracket\rangle

(45)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\llbracket\pi_{i}\ M\rrbracket\coloneqq\pi_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\delta(M)\rrbracket\coloneqq{?}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\iota_{i}\ M\rrbracket\coloneqq\iota_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket

(46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\llbracket\delta(M,x_{1}.M_{1},x_{2}.M_{2})\rrbracket\coloneqq[\mathcal{A}\llbracket M_{1}\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket M_{2}\rrbracket]\mathrel{\circ}[\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{1},\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{2}]^{-1}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle

(47)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\llbracket\lambda x.M\rrbracket\coloneqq\Lambda(\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket)\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\ N\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\rangle\qquad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathbf{fix}\ f.M\rrbracket\coloneqq(\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket)^{\dagger}

Appendix B
Details of Refinement Type System

B.1.
Formulas

B.1.1.
Well-formed formulas

We define
well-formed formulas

\Gamma\vdash\phi
as follows.

\frac{\mathbf{ap}:\sigma\quad\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma}{\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{ap}(M)}\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash\top}\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash\bot}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\psi\quad\Gamma\vdash\phi}{\Gamma\vdash\psi\implies\phi}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\psi\quad\Gamma\vdash\phi}{\Gamma\vdash\psi\land\phi}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\psi\quad\Gamma\vdash\phi}{\Gamma\vdash\psi\lor\phi}

B.1.2.
Semantics

We assume that readers are familiar with fibrations.
See
(
,
)
for the introduction to fibrations.

Notations:
Let
p:\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{B}
be a fibration.

•

\mathbb{E}_{I}
is the fibre category over
I\in\mathbb{B}
.

•

The reindexing functor along
u:I\to J
is denoted by
u^{*}:\mathbb{E}_{J}\to\mathbb{E}_{I}
.

•

A cartesian lifting over
u:I\to J
is denoted by
\overline{u}(Y):u^{*}Y\to Y
where
Y\in\mathbb{E}_{J}
.

We define a fibration
p:\mathbb{P}\to\mathbb{C}
by the change-of-base construction.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}(\mathbf{Set})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Set}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{sub}_{\mathbb{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}(1,{-})}

An object in
\mathbb{P}
is a pair
(I,P)
such that
I\in\mathbb{C}
and
P\subseteq\mathbb{C}(1,I)
and a morphism
f:(I,P)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}(J,Q)
is a morphism
f:I\to J
such that for any
x\in P
,
f\mathrel{\circ}x\in Q
.
We sometimes omit
I
and write
P=(I,P)
when
I
is clear from the context.

Lemma B.1.

p:\mathbb{P}\to\mathbb{C}
is a bifibration with a fibred bi-cc structure and simple products/coproducts.

Proof.

The subobject fibration
\mathbf{Sub}(\mathbf{Set})\to\mathbf{Set}
is a bifibration with a fibred bi-cc structure and simple products/coproducts, and
U
preserves finite products (see
(
Hermida, 1993
, 4.3.1)
and
(
Jacobs, 2001
, Lemma 1.9.14)
).
∎

Notation:

•

Fibred initial/terminal object functor are
\bot,\top:\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{P}
.

•

A fibred product, a fibred coproduct, and a fibred exponential for
X,Y\in\mathbb{P}_{I}
are denoted by
X\land Y
,
X\lor Y
,
X\implies Y
.

•

For simple products and coproducts, we write
\forall,\exists:\mathbb{P}_{X\times Y}\to\mathbb{P}_{X}
.

Definition B.2 (interpretation of formulas).

Let
\mathcal{P}
be an interpretation of atomic predicates such that
\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{ap})\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathrm{ar}(\mathbf{ap})\rrbracket}
(i.e.
\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{ap})\subseteq\mathbb{C}(1,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathrm{ar}(\mathbf{ap})\rrbracket)
) for any atomic predicate
\mathbf{ap}:\mathrm{ar}(\mathbf{ap})\in\mathbf{AP}
.
For any well-formed formula
\Gamma\vdash\phi
, we define
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\phi\rrbracket
as follows.

(48)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{ap}(M)\rrbracket\coloneqq(\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket)^{*}\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{ap})=\{\gamma:1\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\mid\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\gamma\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{ap})\}

(49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\top\rrbracket\coloneqq\top\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\qquad\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\bot\rrbracket\coloneqq\bot\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket

(50)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\psi\land\phi\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\psi\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\phi\rrbracket\qquad\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\psi\lor\phi\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\psi\rrbracket\lor\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(51)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\psi\implies\phi\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\psi\rrbracket\implies\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\phi\rrbracket

B.2.
Well-Formed Contexts/Types

We define well-formed contexts
\vdash\dot{\Gamma}
and well-formed types
\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}
inductively so that any formula in
\dot{\Gamma}
and
\dot{\sigma}
is well-formed.

\frac{}{\vdash\cdot}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}}{\vdash\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}}

\frac{\vdash\dot{\Gamma}\quad|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\sigma\vdash\phi\quad\sigma\in\mathbf{Base}\cup\{\mathbf{Prop},1\}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v:\sigma\mid\phi\}}\frac{\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}}\frac{\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}}\frac{}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash 0}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}+\dot{\tau}}

B.3.
Semantics of Well-Formed Contexts/Types

Let
s_{\mathbb{C}}:s(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
be the simple fibration on
\mathbb{C}
(see
(
,
)
for the definition).
Since
\mathbb{C}
is cartesian closed,
s_{\mathbb{C}}:s(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
is a CCompC
(
Jacobs, 2001
, Theorem 10.5.5)
.
Since
\mathbb{C}
is a bi-ccc,
s_{\mathbb{C}}:s(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
has strong fibred coproducts.

More concretely,
s_{\mathbb{C}}:s(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
has the following structures.

•

An object in
s(\mathbb{C})
is a pair
(I,X)
where
I,X\in\mathbb{C}
.

•

A morphism in
s(\mathbb{C})
is a pair
(u,f):(I,X)\to(J,Y)
where
u:I\to J
and
f:I\times X\to Y
.

•

Cartesian liftings are given as follows.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}(J,X)=(I,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(J,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}(J,Y)=(I,Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(J,Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u,\pi_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}(\mathrm{id},f)=(\mathrm{id},f\mathrel{\circ}(u\times\mathrm{id}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\mathbb{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u,\pi_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

•

Fibred terminal object functor
1:\mathbb{C}\to s(\mathbb{C})
.

1I=(I,1)

•

Comprehension functor
\{{-}\}:s(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
.

\{(I,X)\}=I\times X\qquad\mathbb{C}(I,\{(J,Y)\})\cong s_{\mathbb{C}}(1I,(J,Y))

•

Product
\prod_{(I,X)}:(s_{\mathbb{C}})_{I\times X}\to(s_{\mathbb{C}})_{I}
.

\prod_{(I,X)}(I\times X,Y)=(I,X\Rightarrow Y)\qquad\prod_{(I,X)}(\mathrm{id}_{I\times X},f)=(\mathrm{id},\Lambda(f\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{1}\times\mathrm{id},\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})\rangle))

(s_{\mathbb{C}})_{I\times X}(\pi_{1}^{*}(I,Z),(I\times X,Y))\cong(s_{\mathbb{C}})_{I}((I,Z),\prod_{(I,X)}(I\times X,Y))

\eta=\Lambda(\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):(I,Y)\to(I,X\Rightarrow Y)

\epsilon=(\mathrm{id},\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\sigma\mathrel{\circ}(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})):(I\times X,X\Rightarrow Y)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}(I\times X,Y)

•

Coproduct

\coprod_{(I,X)}(I\times X,Y)=(I,X\times Y)\qquad\coprod_{(I,X)}(\mathrm{id}_{I\times X},f)=(\mathrm{id}_{I},)

(s_{\mathbb{C}})_{I\times X}((I\times X,Y),\pi_{1}^{*}(I,Z))\cong(s_{\mathbb{C}})_{I}(\coprod_{(I,X)}(I\times X,Y),(I,Z))

\eta=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id}):(I\times X,Y)\to(I\times X,X\times Y)\qquad\epsilon=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}):(I,X\times Y)\to(I,Y)

\kappa=\alpha:(I\times X)\times Y\to I\times(X\times Y)\qquad\mathbf{fst}=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}):(I,X\times Y)\to(I,X)

•

Strong fibred initial objects
0:\mathbb{C}\to s(\mathbb{C})
.

0I=(I,0)

{?}_{(I,X)}=(\mathrm{id},{?}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}):(I,0)\to(I,X)

•

Strong fibred binary coproducts

(I,X)+(I,Y)=(I,X+Y)

\iota_{1}=(\mathrm{id},\iota_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}):(I,X)\to(I,X+Y)\qquad\iota_{2}=(\mathrm{id},\iota_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}):(I,X)\to(I,X+Y)

The functor

\langle(\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{1})^{*},(\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{2})^{*}\rangle:s(\mathbb{C})_{I\times(X+Y)}\to s(\mathbb{C})_{I\times X}\times s(\mathbb{C})_{I\times Y}

is fully faithful.

By applying the construction in
(
Kura, 2021
)
, we obtain a model of a dependent refinement type system.

Lemma B.3.

We have a SCCompC
\{s_{\mathbb{C}}\mid p\}:\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}\to\mathbb{P}
with strong fibred coproducts.
We also have the following morphism of SCCompCs.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{s_{\mathbb{C}}\mid p\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\mathbb{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Proof.

The side condition for the existence of strong (binary) coproducts is satisfied as follows.

•

s(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
is a CCompC with strong fibred coproducts.

•

For any
u:I\to I^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{C}
and
(I^{\prime},X),(I^{\prime},Y)\in s(\mathbb{C})_{I^{\prime}}
, the following two squares are pullbacks.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\times(X+Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\times Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\prime}\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\prime}\times(X+Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\prime}\times Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{2}}

•

p:\mathbb{P}\to\mathbb{C}
is a fibred bi-ccc and thus satisfies Frobenius by
(
Hermida, 1993
, Remark 4.5.7)
.

•

p:\mathbb{P}\to\mathbb{C}
is a cofibration.

∎

More concretely,
\{s_{\mathbb{C}}\mid p\}:\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}\to\mathbb{P}
has the following structures.

•

An object
((I,X),P,Q)\in\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
consists of
(I,X)\in s(\mathbb{C})
,
P\subseteq\mathbb{C}(1,I)
, and
Q\subseteq\mathbb{C}(1,I\times X)
such that if
\langle i,x\rangle\in Q
, then
i\in P
.

•

A morphism in
\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
(denoted by
(u,f):((I,X),P,Q)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}((J,Y),R,S)
) is a morphism
(u,f):(I,X)\to(J,Y)
in
s(\mathbb{C})
such that
u:P\mathrel{\dot{\to}}R
and
\langle u\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1},f\rangle:Q\mathrel{\dot{\to}}S
in
\mathbb{P}
, that is, for any
i\in P
,
u\mathrel{\circ}i\in R
and for any
\langle i,x\rangle\in Q
,
\langle u\mathrel{\circ}i,f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i,x\rangle\rangle\in R
.

•

Reindexing functor:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((I,X),P,\pi_{(I,X)}^{*}P\land(u\times\mathrm{id})^{*}R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((J,X),Q,R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((I,Y),P,\pi_{(I,Y)}^{*}P\land(u\times\mathrm{id})^{*}S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((J,Y),Q,S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(I,P)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(J,Q)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u,\pi_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},f\mathrel{\circ}(u\times\mathrm{id}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u,\pi_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

•

Terminal object functor

\dot{1}(I,P)=((I,1),P,\pi_{1}^{*}P)

where
\pi_{1}^{*}P=\{\langle i,\mathrm{id}\rangle:1\to I\times 1\mid i\in P\}

•

Comprehension functor

\{((I,X),P,Q)\}=(I\times X,Q)

•

Product

\dot{\prod}_{((I,X),P,Q)}(I\times X,Y,Q,R)=((I,X\Rightarrow Y),P,\text{(deferred to \eqref{eq:prod_pred})})

where the last component is

(52)

\displaystyle\{\langle i,f\rangle:1\to I\times(X\Rightarrow Y)\mid i\in P\land

(53)

\displaystyle\qquad\forall x:1\to X,\langle i,x\rangle\in Q\implies\langle\langle i,x\rangle,\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\langle f,x\rangle\rangle\in R\}

•

Coproduct

\dot{\coprod}_{((I,X),P,Q)}(I\times X,Y,Q,R)=((I,X\times Y),P,(\alpha^{-1})^{*}R)

where
(\alpha^{-1})^{*}R=\{\langle i,\langle x,y\rangle\rangle:1\to I\times(X\times Y)\mid\langle\langle i,x\rangle,y\rangle\in R\}

•

Fibred binary coproduct

(54)

\displaystyle((I,X),P,Q)\mathrel{\dot{+}}((I,Y),P,R)

(55)

\displaystyle=((I,X+Y),P,\{(\mathrm{id}_{I}\times\iota_{1})\mathrel{\circ}f\mid f\in Q\}\cup\{(\mathrm{id}_{I}\times\iota_{2})\mathrel{\circ}g\mid g\in R\})

•

Fibred initial object

\dot{0}(I,P)=((I,0),P,\emptyset)

Note that morphisms in
\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
and
\mathbb{P}
are denoted by
(u,f):((I,X),P,Q)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}((J,Y),R,S)
and
u:(I,P)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}(J,R)
, which indicate that they are unique morphisms over
(u,f):(I,X)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}(J,Y)
in
s(\mathbb{C})
and
u:I\mathrel{\dot{\to}}J
in
\mathbb{C}
, respectively.

Definition B.4 (interpretation of well-formed contexts/types).

A well-formed context
\vdash\dot{\Gamma}
is interpreted as
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\rrbracket}
.

(56)

\displaystyle\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket\coloneqq\top 1\in\mathbb{P}_{1}\qquad\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\coloneqq\{\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\}\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\rrbracket\times\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket}

A well-formed type
\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}
is interpreted as
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\in\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
such that
u\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket=(\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket)
.

(57)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v:\sigma\mid\phi\}\rrbracket\coloneqq((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\sigma\vdash\phi\rrbracket)

(58)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket\coloneqq\dot{\prod}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(59)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}\rrbracket\coloneqq\dot{\coprod}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket

(60)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash 0\rrbracket\coloneqq\dot{0}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\qquad\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}+\dot{\tau}\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{+}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket

B.4.
Subtyping relation

We define
\dot{\Gamma}\mid v:\sigma.\phi\vDash\psi
by

\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\sigma\vdash\phi\rrbracket\leq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\sigma\vdash\psi\rrbracket.

Intuitively, this means that if formulas in
\dot{\Gamma}
and
\phi
are true, then
\psi
is also true.

We define a subtyping relation
\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}<:\dot{\tau}
.

\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}<:\dot{\sigma}}\quad\textsc{Sub-Refl}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}<:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}<:\dot{\sigma}_{3}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}<:\dot{\sigma}_{3}}\quad\textsc{Sub-Trans}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash v:\sigma.\phi\vDash\psi\quad\sigma\in\mathbf{Base}\cup\{\mathbf{Prop},1\}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v:\sigma\mid\phi\}<:\{v:\sigma\mid\psi\}}\quad\textsc{Sub-Refine}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}<:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\quad\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash\tau_{1}\quad\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}<:\dot{\tau}_{2}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma}_{1})\to\dot{\tau}_{1}<:(x:\dot{\sigma}_{2})\to\dot{\tau}_{2}}\quad\textsc{Sub-DFun}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}<:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\quad\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\tau_{2}\quad\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}<:\dot{\tau}_{2}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma}_{1})\times\dot{\tau}_{1}<:(x:\dot{\sigma}_{2})\times\dot{\tau}_{2}}\quad\textsc{Sub-DPair}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}<:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}<:\dot{\tau}_{2}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}+\dot{\tau}_{1}<:\dot{\sigma}_{2}+\dot{\tau}_{2}}\quad\textsc{Sub-Sum}

B.5.
Typing rules

A well-typed term
\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\sigma}
is defined as follows.

\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\sigma}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}<:\dot{\tau}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\tau}}\quad\textsc{R-Sub}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash N:\dot{\sigma}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M\ N:\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi[N/x]\}}\quad\textsc{R-App}\frac{\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash M:\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\lambda x:\dot{\sigma}.M:(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}}\quad\textsc{R-Abs}\frac{\vdash\dot{\Gamma}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash():\{v:1\mid\top\}}\quad\textsc{R-Unit}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\sigma}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash N:\dot{\tau}[M/x]}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(M,N):(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}}\quad\textsc{R-Pair}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\pi_{1}\ M:\dot{\sigma}}\quad\textsc{R-Fst}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\pi_{2}\ M:\dot{\tau}[\pi_{1}\ M/x]}\quad\textsc{R-Snd}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:0\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\delta(M):\dot{\tau}}\quad\textsc{R-Case0}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\sigma}_{i}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{3-i}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\iota_{i}\ M:\dot{\sigma}_{1}+\dot{\sigma}_{2}}\quad\textsc{R-Inj}\frac{\dot{\Gamma},z:\dot{\sigma}_{1}+\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\sigma}_{1}+\dot{\sigma}_{2}\quad\dot{\Gamma},x_{1}:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash N_{1}:\dot{\tau}[\iota_{1}\ x_{1}/z]\quad\dot{\Gamma},x_{2}:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash N_{2}:\dot{\tau}[\iota_{2}\ x_{2}/z]}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\delta(M,x_{1}.N_{1},x_{2}.N_{2}):\dot{\tau}[M/z]}\quad\textsc{R-Case2}\frac{\vdash\dot{\Gamma}\quad(x:\{v:b\mid\phi\})\in\dot{\Gamma}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash x:\{v:b\mid v=x\}}\quad\textsc{R-VarRefine}\frac{\vdash\dot{\Gamma}\quad(x:\dot{\sigma})\in\dot{\Gamma}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash x:\dot{\sigma}}\quad\textsc{R-Var}\frac{\dot{\Gamma},f:(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\vdash M:(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\quad|\dot{\Gamma}|,x:|\dot{\sigma}|,v:\mathbf{Prop}\vdash\phi\quad\text{$\phi$ is admissible at $v$}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\mathbf{fix}\ f.M:(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}}\quad\textsc{R-Fix}\frac{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\sigma}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\quad\mathrm{ar}(\mathbf{op})=|\dot{\sigma}|\quad\mathrm{car}(\mathbf{op})=|\dot{\tau}|\quad(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{op})):\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\mathbf{op}(M):\dot{\tau}}\quad\textsc{R-BasicOp}

\frac{\mathbf{op}:1\to\tau\quad\tau\in\mathbf{Base}\cup\{\mathbf{Prop}\}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\mathbf{op}:\{v:\tau\mid v=_{\tau}\mathbf{op}\}}\quad\textsc{R-BasicConst}\frac{\mathbf{op}:\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\to\tau\quad\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{n},\tau\in\mathbf{Base}\cup\{\mathbf{Prop}\}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\{(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\mid\phi[\mathbf{op}(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})/v]\}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\mathbf{op}(M):\{v:\tau\mid\phi\}}\quad\textsc{R-BasicSimple}\frac{\mathbf{op}:\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\to 1+1\quad\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{n}\in\mathbf{Base}\cup\{\mathbf{Prop}\}\quad v_{1}:\sigma_{1},\dots,v_{n}:\sigma_{n}\vdash\psi_{t}\quad\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\vdash\psi_{t}\rrbracket\subseteq\pi_{2}^{*}a(\mathbf{op})^{*}\{\iota_{1}\}\quad v_{1}:\sigma_{1},\dots,v_{n}:\sigma_{n}\vdash\psi_{f}\quad\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\vdash\psi_{f}\rrbracket\subseteq\pi_{2}^{*}a(\mathbf{op})^{*}\{\iota_{2}\}\quad\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\{(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\mid\psi_{t}\land\phi_{t}[()/v]\lor\psi_{f}\land\phi_{f}[()/v]\}}{\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\mathbf{op}(M):\{v:1\mid\phi_{t}\}+\{v:1\mid\phi_{f}\}}\quad\textsc{R-BasicBool}

Appendix C
Details of Proofs

C.1.
Preparation

Definition C.1.

Let
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
be contexts and
\sigma
be a type.
We define
\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma_{1};\sigma;\Gamma_{2}}:\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\rrbracket\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\rrbracket
as follows.

\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma_{1};\sigma;\cdot}\coloneqq\pi_{1}\qquad\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma_{1};\sigma;\Gamma_{2},y:\tau}\coloneqq\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma_{1};\sigma;\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{id}

Lemma C.2 (weakening for terms).

For any well-typed term
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash M:\tau
and type
\sigma
,

\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash M:\tau\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma_{1};\sigma;\Gamma_{2}}=\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash M:\tau\rrbracket

Lemma C.3 (weakening for formulas).

For any well-formed
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash\phi
and type
\sigma
,

\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma_{1};\sigma;\Gamma_{2}}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket

Proof.

By induction.

•

For basic predicates including
\leq_{\mathbf{Prop}}
and
=_{b}
,

(61)

\displaystyle\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma_{1};\sigma;\Gamma_{2}}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash a(M)\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma_{1};\sigma;\Gamma_{2}}^{*}(\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash M:\tau\rrbracket)^{*}\mathcal{P}(a)

(62)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash M:\tau\rrbracket)^{*}\mathcal{P}(a)

(63)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash a(M)\rrbracket

by Lemma
C.2
.

•

For
\bot,\top,\lor,\land,\implies
, we use the fact that fibred bi-cc structures are preserved by reindexing.

∎

Lemma C.4 (weakening for types).

Let
\dot{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\sigma}
be a well-formed type.

•

For any well-formed context
\vdash\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}
, we have

\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket.

•

For any well-formed type
\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}
, we have

\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket.

Proof.

By simultaneous induction on
\vdash\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}
and
\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}
.

•

If
\dot{\Gamma}_{2}=\cdot
, we have

\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;\cdot}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1}\rrbracket

because
\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;\cdot}=\pi_{1}=\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
.

•

If
\dot{\Gamma}_{2}=\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2},y:\dot{\sigma}^{\prime}
, we have
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2},y:\dot{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}\vdash\dot{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2},y:\dot{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\overline{\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}|}}(\dots)\}}

(64)

\displaystyle\{\overline{\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}}(\dots)\}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}\times\mathrm{id}

(65)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,y:|\dot{\sigma}^{\prime}|}

•

\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\{v:\tau\mid\phi\}
:
By definition of the interpretation,

(66)

\displaystyle\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\{v:\tau\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(67)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket,\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket)

(68)

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket,

(69)

\displaystyle\qquad\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket\land(\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket)

and by Lemma
C.3
,

(70)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(71)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{proj}_{|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|;|\dot{\sigma}|;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(72)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket

•

\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash(x:\dot{\tau}_{1})\to\{v:\tau\mid\phi\}
and
\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash(x:\dot{\tau}_{1})\times\dot{\tau}_{2}
: By the BC condition.

•

\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash 0
and
\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}+\dot{\tau}_{2}
: Because reindexing functors preserve fibred coproducts.

∎

Definition C.5.

Let
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
be contexts and
\Gamma_{1}\vdash M:\sigma
be a well-typed term.
We define
\mathrm{subst}_{M;\Gamma_{2}}:\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\rrbracket\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\rrbracket
as follows.

\mathrm{subst}_{M;\cdot}\coloneqq\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle\qquad\mathrm{subst}_{M;\Gamma_{2},y:\tau}\coloneqq\mathrm{subst}_{M;\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{id}

Lemma C.6.

For any well-typed terms
\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash N
and
\Gamma_{1}\vdash M:\sigma
, we have

\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\mathrm{subst}_{M;\Gamma_{2}}=\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash N[M/x]\rrbracket

Proof.

By induction on
N
.
∎

Lemma C.7 (substitution in formulas).

For any well-formed
\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash\phi
and
\Gamma_{1}\vdash M:\sigma
,

\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash\phi[M/x]\rrbracket=\mathrm{subst}_{M;\Gamma_{2}}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket

Proof.

Similarly to Lemma
C.3

•

For basic predicates including
\leq_{\mathbf{Prop}}
and
=_{b}
,

(73)

\displaystyle\mathrm{subst}_{M;\Gamma_{2}}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash a(N)\rrbracket

(74)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{subst}_{M;\Gamma_{2}}^{*}(\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1}.x:\sigma,\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\tau\rrbracket)^{*}\mathcal{P}(a)

(75)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash N[M/x]:\tau\rrbracket)^{*}\mathcal{P}(a)

(76)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash a(N)[M/x]\rrbracket

by Lemma
C.2
.

•

For
\bot,\top,\lor,\land,\implies
, we use the fact that fibred bi-cc structures are preserved by reindexing.

∎

Lemma C.8 (substitution in types).

For any well-formed type
\dot{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\sigma}
and well-typed term
|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|\vdash M:|\dot{\sigma}|
, if
\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1}\rrbracket\to\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket
in
\mathbb{P}
, then

•

For any well-formed context
\vdash\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}
,

\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket\qquad\text{in $\mathbb{P}$}

•

For any well-formed type
\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}
,

\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\vdash\dot{\tau}[M/x]\rrbracket

Proof.

By simultaneous induction on
\vdash\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}
and
\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}
.

•

If
\dot{\Gamma}_{2}=\cdot
, then we have
\mathrm{subst}_{M;\cdot}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket
by assumption.

•

If
\dot{\Gamma}_{2}=\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2},y:\dot{\tau}
, then
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}[M/x],y:\dot{\tau}[M/x]\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2},y:\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\overline{\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}|}}(\dots)\}}

\{\overline{\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}|}}(\dots)\}=\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}|}\times\mathrm{id}=\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2},y:\dot{\tau}|}

•

\{v:\tau\mid\phi\}
: By the definition of reindexing in
\{s_{\mathbb{C}}\mid p\}:\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}\to\mathbb{P}
.

(77)

\displaystyle\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\{v:\tau\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(78)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket,

(79)

\displaystyle\qquad\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|,x:|\dot{\sigma}|,|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket)

(80)

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]|\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket,

(81)

\displaystyle\qquad\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket\land(\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|,x:|\dot{\sigma}|,|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket))

(82)

\displaystyle\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket\land(\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|,x:|\dot{\sigma}|,|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket)

(83)

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket\land\pi_{1}^{*}\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket\land\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|,x:|\dot{\sigma}|,|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(84)

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}_{1}|,|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi[M/x]\rrbracket

Here, we used Lemma
C.7
and
|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]|=|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|
.

•

(y:\dot{\tau})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}
: Apply the BC condition.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x],y:\dot{\tau}[M/x]\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2},y:\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,y:|\dot{\tau}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}}

(85)

\displaystyle\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash(y:\dot{\tau})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(86)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2},y:\dot{\tau}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(87)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\vdash\dot{\tau}[M/x]\rrbracket}\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|,y:|\dot{\tau}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2},y:\dot{\tau}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(88)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\vdash\dot{\tau}[M/x]\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x],y:\dot{\tau}[M/x]\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi[M/x]\}\rrbracket

(89)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\vdash(y:\dot{\tau}[M/x])\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi[M/x]\}\rrbracket

•

(y:\dot{\tau}_{1})\times\dot{\tau}_{2}
: Apply the BC condition (almost the same as above).

•

0
: Fibred initial objects are preserved by reindexing functors.

(90)

\displaystyle\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash 0\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}0\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\rrbracket

(91)

\displaystyle=0\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket

(92)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\vdash 0\rrbracket

•

\dot{\tau}_{1}+\dot{\tau}_{2}
: Fibred binary coproducts are preserved by reindexing functors.

(93)

\displaystyle\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}+\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

(94)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket+\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket)

(95)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket+\mathrm{subst}_{M;|\dot{\Gamma}_{2}|}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},x:\dot{\sigma},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

(96)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}[M/x]\rrbracket+\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}[M/x]\rrbracket

(97)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}_{1},\dot{\Gamma}_{2}[M/x]\vdash(\dot{\tau}_{1}+\dot{\tau}_{2})[M/x]\rrbracket

∎

C.2.
Proofs for Subtyping Rules

Proposition C.9 (soundness of subtyping).

For any
\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}<:\dot{\tau}
, we have

\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\leq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket

where we define
((I,X),P,Q)\leq((I,X),P,Q^{\prime})
if
Q\subseteq Q^{\prime}
, or equivalently,
\mathrm{id}_{(I,X)}:((I,X),P,Q)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}((I,X),P,Q^{\prime})
.

Proof.

By induction on derivation of
\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}<:\dot{\tau}
.

•

Sub-Refl, Sub-Trans: By reflexivity and transitivity of
\subseteq
.

•

Sub-Refine: Obvious from the definition of the interpretation.

•

Sub-Sum: Easy.

•

Sub-DFun:
By IH, we have

(98)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}\rrbracket\leq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}\rrbracket

(99)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket\leq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

We apply the BC condition.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}_{\{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}\rrbracket\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}_{\{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}\rrbracket\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\mathrm{id}\}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod}

(100)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma}_{1})\to\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket

(101)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}\rrbracket}\{\mathrm{id}\}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket

(102)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket

(103)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

(104)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma}_{2})\to\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

•

Sub-DPair:
By IH, we have

(105)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}\rrbracket\leq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}\rrbracket

(106)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket\leq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

We apply the BC condition.

(107)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma}_{1})\times\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{1}\rrbracket

(108)

\displaystyle\leq\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{1}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

(109)

\displaystyle=\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{1}\rrbracket}\{\mathrm{id}\}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

(110)

\displaystyle=\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}_{2}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}_{2}\vdash\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

(111)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma}_{2})\times\dot{\tau}_{2}\rrbracket

∎

C.3.
Proofs for Typing Rules

Theorem C.10 (soundness).

For any well-typed term
\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\sigma}
,

(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket

Note that this is equivalent to the following.

\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\}

Proof.

By induction on derivation of
\dot{\Gamma}\vdash M:\dot{\sigma}
.
The basic idea of the proof is to interpret terms in
\{s_{\mathbb{C}}\mid p\}:\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}\to\mathbb{P}
as terms of a dependent type system.
Proofs for individual cases are given below.

R-Sub
: Lemma
C.11

•

C.12

•

: Lemma
C.13

•

•

: Lemma
C.15

•

: Lemma
C.16

•

R-Snd
: Lemma
C.17

•

R-Case0
: Lemma
C.18

•

R-Inj
: Lemma
C.19

•

R-Case2
: Lemma
C.20

•

: Lemma
C.21

•

: Lemma
C.22

•

R-Fix
: Lemma
C.29

•

R-BasicOp
: Lemma
C.30

∎

Lemma C.11.

R-Sub
is sound.

Proof.

By IH and Proposition
C.9
, we have

(112)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket

(113)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\leq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket.

Therefore, we have

(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket.

∎

Lemma C.12.

R-App
is sound.

Proof.

By IH, we have

(114)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(115)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket

We consider the following vertical morphism in
\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\rangle^{*}\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}^{*}\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\rangle^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi[N/x]\}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\rangle^{*}\epsilon^{\pi^{*}\dashv\prod}}
by Lemma
C.8

The composite is equal to
(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\ N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\to\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi[N/x]\}\rrbracket
.

(116)

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\rangle^{*}(\mathrm{id},\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\sigma\mathrel{\circ}(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id}))\mathrel{\circ}(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(117)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\sigma\mathrel{\circ}(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}(\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\rangle\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{1},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle)

(118)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\rangle\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(119)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\ N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

∎

Lemma C.13.

R-Abs
is sound.

Proof.

By IH, we have

(120)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

We consider the following vertical morphism in
\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}^{*}1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\pi^{*}\dashv\prod}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})}

The composite is equal to
(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\lambda x.M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})
.

(121)

\displaystyle\prod(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})\mathrel{\circ}\eta^{\pi^{*}\dashv\prod}

(122)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\Lambda(\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{1}\times\mathrm{id},\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})\rangle))\mathrel{\circ}\eta^{\pi^{*}\dashv\prod}

(123)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\Lambda(\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket)\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})\mathrel{\circ}(\mathrm{id},\dots)

(124)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\Lambda(\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket)\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(125)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\lambda x.M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

∎

Lemma C.14.

R-Unit
is sound.

Proof.

Note that we have

\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v:1\mid\top\}\rrbracket=1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket

Thus,

(\mathrm{id},{!}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})={!}:1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\to 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket

∎

Lemma C.15.

R-Pair
is sound.

Proof.

By IH, we have

(126)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket

(127)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}[M/x]\rrbracket

We consider the following vertical morphism in
\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}[M/x]\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}^{*}\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})}
by Lemma
C.8
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}\eta^{\coprod\dashv\pi^{*}}}

The composite is equal to
(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket(M,N)\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})
.

(128)

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}\eta^{\coprod\dashv\pi^{*}}\mathrel{\circ}(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(129)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}(\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{1},\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle)

(130)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\langle\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket N\rrbracket\rangle\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(131)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket(M,N)\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

∎

Lemma C.16.

R-Fst
is sound.

Proof.

By IH, we have

(132)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}\rrbracket

We consider the following composite.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{fst}}

This is equal to
(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\pi_{1}\ M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})
.

(133)

\displaystyle\mathbf{fst}\mathrel{\circ}(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{1},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle)

(134)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(135)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\pi_{1}\ M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

∎

Lemma C.17.

R-Snd
is sound.

Proof.

By IH, we have

(136)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\times\dot{\tau}\rrbracket

We consider the following composite.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}(\kappa^{-1})^{*}1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma},y:\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}(\kappa^{-1})^{*}\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}^{*}\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma},y:\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}(\kappa^{-1})^{*}\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\pi_{1}\ M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}[\pi_{1}\ M/x]\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}(\kappa^{-1})^{*}\eta^{\coprod\dashv\pi^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}(\kappa^{-1})^{*}\pi^{*}\mathbf{fst}}
by Lemma
C.8

Here, we used the following equation.

(137)

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\circ}\kappa^{-1}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle

(138)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle

(139)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\pi_{1}\ M\rrbracket\rangle

The composite above is equal to
(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\pi_{2}\ M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})
.

(140)

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}(\kappa^{-1})^{*}\pi^{*}\mathbf{fst}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle^{*}(\kappa^{-1})^{*}\eta^{\coprod\dashv\pi^{*}}

(141)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}(\langle\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle\times\mathrm{id}))\mathrel{\circ}(\mathrm{id},(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}((\alpha^{-1}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\rangle)\times\mathrm{id}))

(142)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2})\mathrel{\circ}(\mathrm{id},(\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket)\times\mathrm{id})

(143)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{1},(\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket)\times\mathrm{id}\rangle)

(144)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(145)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\pi_{2}\ M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

∎

, we have the following for
