Lemma C.21.

R-Var
is sound.

Proof.

By induction on the context.

•

If
\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash x:\dot{\sigma}
, then we prove

(\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket.

Consider the following.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\coprod\dashv\pi^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}\mathbf{fst}}
by Lemma
C.4

This is equal to
(\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})
.

(168)

\displaystyle\pi^{*}\mathbf{fst}\mathrel{\circ}\eta^{\coprod\dashv\pi^{*}}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}(\pi_{1}\times\mathrm{id}))\mathrel{\circ}(\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})

(169)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}(\pi_{1}\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id}\rangle)

(170)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

•

If
\dot{\Gamma},y:\dot{\tau}\vdash x:\dot{\sigma}
and
(x:\dot{\sigma})\in\dot{\Gamma}
, then we prove

(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,y:|\dot{\tau}|\vdash x:|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},y:\dot{\tau}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},y:\dot{\tau}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket

where IH is given as follows.

(\mathrm{id},\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash x:|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket

Consider the following.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},y:\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}^{*}1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},y:\dot{\tau}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}(\mathrm{id},\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash x:|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})}
by Lemma
C.4

This is equal to
(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,y:|\dot{\tau}|\vdash x:|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})
.

(171)

\displaystyle\pi^{*}(\mathrm{id},\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash x:|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(172)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash x:|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}(\pi_{1}\times\mathrm{id}))

(173)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash x:|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(174)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id},\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma},y:|\dot{\tau}||\vdash x:|\dot{\sigma}|\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

∎

Lemma C.22.

R-VarRefine
is sound.

Proof.

•

If
\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\vdash x:\{v:b\mid v=x\}
, then we prove

(\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\vdash\{v:b\mid v=x\}\rrbracket.

Since

(175)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\vdash\{v:b\mid v=x\}\rrbracket

(176)

\displaystyle=((\dots,\dots),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket,

(177)

\displaystyle\qquad\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,x:b,v:b\vdash v=x\rrbracket)

(178)

\displaystyle=((\dots,\dots),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket,

(179)

\displaystyle\qquad\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket\land\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle^{*}\mathcal{P}({=}))

it suffices to prove

\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket\leq\langle\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle^{*}(\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket\land\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle^{*}\mathcal{P}({=}))

This follows from the following equations.

\langle\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle^{*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket=\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{v:b\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(180)

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle^{*}\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle^{*}\mathcal{P}({=})

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}^{*}\pi_{2}^{*}\langle\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}\rangle^{*}\mathcal{P}({=})

(181)

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}^{*}\pi_{2}^{*}\top(Ab)

(182)

\displaystyle=\top\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,x:b,v:b\rrbracket

•

If
\dot{\Gamma},y:\dot{\tau}\vdash x:\{v:b\mid v=x\}
, then the proof is the same as R-Var.

∎

Definition C.23.

Let
\Gamma=x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\sigma_{n}
be a context and
\gamma\in 1\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
For each
f:1\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{i}\rrbracket
, we define
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f]:1\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
by

\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f]\coloneqq\langle\dots\langle\mathrm{id},\gamma_{1}\rangle,\dots,f,\dots,\gamma_{n}\rangle

where
\gamma=\langle\dots\langle\mathrm{id},\gamma_{1}\rangle,\dots,\gamma_{i},\dots,\gamma_{n}\rangle
.

Definition C.24.

Let
\Gamma=x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\sigma_{n}
be a context.
We say
Q\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket}
is
admissible at variable
x_{i}
if

•

\mathbb{C}(1,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{i}\rrbracket)
has a bottom element
\bot_{\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{i}\rrbracket}

•

for any
\gamma:1\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
, we have
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\bot_{\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{i}\rrbracket}]\in Q

•

for any
\gamma:1\to\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
and any
\omega
-chain
f_{0}\leq f_{1}\leq\dots
in
\mathbb{C}(1,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{i}\rrbracket)
, if
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f_{k}]\in Q
holds for each
k
, then
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{k}f_{k}]\in Q
(i.e. Q is chain-closed at
x_{i}
)

Definition C.25.

((I,X),P,Q)\in\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
is
admissible
if

•

X
is an EM
T
-algebra (which implies that
\mathbb{C}(1,X)
has a bottom element
\bot_{X}:1\to X
)

•

for each
i\in P
,
\langle i,\bot_{X}\rangle\in Q

•

for any
i\in P
and
\omega
-chain
f_{0}\leq f_{1}\leq\dots
such that
\langle i,f_{n}\rangle\in Q
for any
n
, we have
\langle i,\sup_{n}f_{n}\rangle\in Q

Lemma C.26.

If
\vdash\dot{\Gamma}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\mathbf{Prop}\vdash\phi\rrbracket
is admissible at
v
, then
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket
is admissible.

Proof.

By definition,

\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket=((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\rrbracket,\Omega),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\mathbf{Prop}\vdash\phi\rrbracket)

is admissible.
∎

Lemma C.27.

Let
((I,X),P,Q)\in\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
.
If
((I\times X,Y),Q,R)\in\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
is admissible, then
\dot{\prod}_{((I,X),P,Q)}((I\times X,Y),Q,R)
is admissible.

Proof.

Note that we have
\bot_{X\Rightarrow Y}=\Lambda(\bot_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})
.

(183)

\displaystyle\bot_{X\Rightarrow Y}

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\nu_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}T\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T})\mathrel{\circ}T{?}_{X\Rightarrow Y}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T0}

(184)

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\nu_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}T\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}T({?}_{X\Rightarrow Y}\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T}\mathrel{\circ}(\bot_{T0}\times\mathrm{id}))

(185)

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\nu_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}T\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}T(\Lambda({?}_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T}\mathrel{\circ}(\bot_{T0}\times\mathrm{id}))

(186)

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\nu_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}T{?}_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}T\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T}\mathrel{\circ}(\bot_{T0}\times\mathrm{id}))

(187)

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\nu_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}T{?}_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}(\bot_{T0}\times\mathrm{id}))

(188)

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\nu_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}T{?}_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T0}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

(189)

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\bot_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})

So, (
53
) satisfies the second condition of Def.
C.25
because for each
x:1\to X
, if
\langle i,x\rangle\in Q
, then

\langle\langle i,x\rangle,\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\bot_{X\Rightarrow Y},x\rangle\rangle=\langle\langle i,x\rangle,\bot_{Y}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathrm{id},x\rangle\rangle=\langle\langle i,x\rangle,\bot_{Y}\rangle\in R.

It is routine to check that (
53
) satisfies the third condition of Def.
C.25
.
∎

Lemma C.28.

Let
A
be an EM
T
-algebra for a pseudo-lifting strong monad
T
with structure map
\nu:TA\to A
.
If
((I,A),P,Q)\in\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}
is admissible and
(\mathrm{id},f):((I,A),P,Q)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}((I,A),P,Q)
, then
(\mathrm{id},f^{\dagger}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1(I,P)\mathrel{\dot{\to}}((I,A),P,Q)

Proof.

It suffices to show
\langle\mathrm{id},f^{\dagger}\rangle:P\mathrel{\dot{\to}}Q
(that is,
\langle i,f^{\dagger}\mathrel{\circ}i\rangle\in Q
for any
i\in P
) under the assumption that we have
\langle\pi_{1},f\rangle:Q\mathrel{\dot{\to}}Q
.

Recall that
f^{\dagger}
is defined by

f^{\dagger}\coloneqq\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\sup_{n}\lceil f\rceil^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)}\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle

where

(190)

\displaystyle\lceil f\rceil

\displaystyle\coloneqq\eta^{T}\mathrel{\circ}\Lambda(f\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{2},\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\rangle):T(I\Rightarrow A)\to T(I\Rightarrow A)

(191)

\displaystyle\nu_{X}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Lambda(\nu\mathrel{\circ}T\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T}):T(I\Rightarrow A)\to I\Rightarrow A

We show

\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\lceil f\rceil^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)}\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle\mathrel{\circ}i=(f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle)^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}

by induction on
n
.

•

Base case:
Note that we have
\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)}=T{?}_{I\Rightarrow A}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{0}
and
{?}_{I\Rightarrow A}=\Lambda({?}_{A}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})
.

(192)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)}\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle\mathrel{\circ}i

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrel{\circ}T\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T}\mathrel{\circ}\langle T{?}_{I\Rightarrow A}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{0}\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle\mathrel{\circ}i

(193)

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrel{\circ}T\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T}\mathrel{\circ}\langle T\Lambda({?}_{A}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{0},i\rangle

(194)

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrel{\circ}T({?}_{A}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\bot_{0},i\rangle

(195)

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrel{\circ}T{?}_{A}\mathrel{\circ}T\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{T}\mathrel{\circ}\langle i,\bot_{0}\rangle

(196)

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrel{\circ}T{?}_{A}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\langle i,\bot_{0}\rangle

(197)

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrel{\circ}T{?}_{A}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{0}

(198)

\displaystyle=\bot_{A}

•

Step case:

(199)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\lceil f\rceil^{n+1}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)}\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle\mathrel{\circ}i

(200)

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrel{\circ}T\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\theta^{\prime T}\mathrel{\circ}(\lceil f\rceil\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\lceil f\rceil^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)},i\rangle

(201)

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrel{\circ}T\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\eta^{T}\mathrel{\circ}(\Lambda(f\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{2},\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\rangle)\times\mathrm{id})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\lceil f\rceil^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)},i\rangle

(202)

\displaystyle=f\mathrel{\circ}\langle\pi_{2},\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\rangle\mathrel{\circ}\langle\lceil f\rceil^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)},i\rangle

(203)

\displaystyle=f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i,\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\lceil f\rceil^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)},i\rangle\rangle

(204)

\displaystyle=f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle\mathrel{\circ}\Lambda^{-1}(\nu_{X})\mathrel{\circ}\langle\lceil f\rceil^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{T(I\Rightarrow A)},i\rangle

Now we prove
\langle i,f^{\dagger}\mathrel{\circ}i\rangle=\langle i,\sup_{n}(f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle)^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}\rangle\in Q
using the admissibility of
((I,A),P,Q)
.
It suffices to prove
\langle i,(f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle)^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}\rangle\in Q
for each
n
.

•

We have
\langle i,\bot_{A}\rangle\in Q
by the definition of the admissibility of
((I,A),P,Q)
.

•

Let
g=f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle
.
If
\langle i,g^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}\rangle\in Q
, then

(205)

\displaystyle\langle i,g^{n+1}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle i,(f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i\mathrel{\circ}{!},\mathrm{id}\rangle)\mathrel{\circ}g^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}\rangle

(206)

\displaystyle=\langle i,f\mathrel{\circ}\langle i,g^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}\rangle\rangle

(207)

\displaystyle=\langle\pi_{1},f\rangle\mathrel{\circ}\langle i,g^{n}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}\rangle

and since we have
\langle\pi_{1},f\rangle:Q\mathrel{\dot{\to}}Q
, it follows that
\langle i,g^{n+1}\mathrel{\circ}\bot_{A}\rangle\in Q
holds.

∎

Lemma C.29.

R-Fix
is sound.

Proof.

By Lemma
C.26
,
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\dot{\sigma}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket
is admissible.
By Lemma
C.27
,
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket
is admissible.
Let
X\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket
.
By IH, we have

(208)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\{X\}\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\pi_{X}^{*}X

Since there is an isomorphism
\mathbb{E}_{\{X\}}(1\{X\},\pi^{*}Y)\cong\mathbb{E}_{I}(X,Y)
for any SCCompC
p:\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{B}
and
X,Y\in\mathbb{E}_{I}
, we have

(209)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket):X\mathrel{\dot{\to}}X

By Lemma
C.28
, we have

(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathbf{fix}\ f.M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})=(\mathrm{id},(\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket)^{\dagger}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash(x:\dot{\sigma})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

∎

Lemma C.30.

R-BasicOp
is sound.

Proof.

We have

(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket\mathbf{op}(M)\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}):1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket

as the following composite.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},\mathcal{A}\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},a(\mathbf{op})\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2})}

Here, the second morphism exists because we have a fully faithful functor
\{s(\mathbb{C})\mid\mathbb{P}\}\to\mathbb{P}^{\to}
.

(210)

\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\sigma}\rrbracket\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id},a(\mathbf{op})\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2})}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\dot{\tau}\rrbracket\qquad\mapsto\qquad\hbox to162.64pt{\vbox to55.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 79.74757pt\lower-26.73174pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

C.4.
Proofs for Specialised Rules for Basic Operators

Corollary C.31.

R-BasicConst
is sound.

Proof.

By Lemma
C.30
, it suffices to prove

(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{op})):\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:1\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{v:\tau\mid v=_{\tau}\mathbf{op}()\}\rrbracket

This follows from the equation below.

(211)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{op}))^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\tau\vdash v=_{\tau}\mathbf{op}()\rrbracket

(212)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{op}))^{*}\langle\pi_{2},a(\mathbf{op})\mathrel{\circ}{!}\rangle^{*}\mathcal{P}({=})

(213)

\displaystyle=\langle a(\mathbf{op})\mathrel{\circ}{!},a(\mathbf{op})\mathrel{\circ}{!}\rangle^{*}\mathcal{P}({=})

(214)

\displaystyle=\top\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:1\rrbracket

∎

Lemma C.32.

Let
\Gamma,x_{1}:\sigma_{1},x_{2}:\sigma_{2}\vdash\phi
be a formula.

\alpha^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma,(x_{1},x_{2}):\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket=\mathcal{A}\llbracket\Gamma,x_{1}:\sigma_{1},x_{2}:\sigma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket

Proof.

By substitution and weakening.

(215)

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma,(x_{1},x_{2}):\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(216)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma,x:\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}\vdash\phi[\pi_{1}\ x/x_{1},\pi_{2}\ x/x_{2}]\rrbracket

(217)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{*}\langle\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\rangle^{*}\langle\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma,(x_{1},x_{2}):\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2},x_{1}:\sigma_{1},x_{2}:\sigma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(218)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{*}\langle\mathrm{id},\pi_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\rangle^{*}\langle\mathrm{id},\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{1}\rangle^{*}((\pi_{1}\times\mathrm{id})\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma,x_{1}:\sigma_{1},x_{2}:\sigma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(219)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma,x_{1}:\sigma_{1},x_{2}:\sigma_{2}\vdash\phi\rrbracket

∎

Lemma C.33.

Let
\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{n}\in B\cup\{1,\mathbf{Prop}\}
.

(220)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(221)

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\rrbracket,\quad\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket\times(\dots\times\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{n}\rrbracket)),\quad\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket,

(222)

\displaystyle\qquad\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,(v_{1},(\dots,v_{n})):\sigma_{1}\times(\dots\times\sigma_{n})\vdash\phi\rrbracket)

Proof.

By induction on
n
.
The base case is trivial.
If
n>1
,

(223)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(224)

\displaystyle=\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v_{1}:\sigma_{1}\mid\top\}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v_{1}:\sigma_{1}\vdash\{(v_{2},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{2}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(225)

\displaystyle=\coprod_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{v_{1}:\sigma_{1}\mid\top\}\rrbracket}((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v_{1}:\sigma_{1}\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{2}\rrbracket\times(\dots\times\mathcal{A}\llbracket\sigma_{n}\rrbracket)),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v_{1}:\sigma_{1}\rrbracket,

(226)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v_{1}:\sigma_{1}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v_{1}:\sigma_{1},(v_{2},(\dots,v_{n})):\sigma_{2}\times(\dots\times\sigma_{n})\vdash\phi\rrbracket)

(227)

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{A}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\rrbracket,\mathcal{A}\llbracket b_{1}\rrbracket\times(\dots\times\mathcal{A}\llbracket b_{n}\rrbracket)),\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket,

(228)

\displaystyle\qquad(\alpha^{-1})^{*}(\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v_{1}:b_{1}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v_{1}:b_{1},(v_{2},(\dots,v_{n})):b_{2}\times(\dots\times b_{n})\vdash\phi\rrbracket))

We have

(\alpha^{-1})^{*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v_{1}:b_{1}\rrbracket=(\alpha^{-1})^{*}\pi^{*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket=\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket

We also have

(229)

\displaystyle(\alpha^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v_{1}:b_{1},(v_{2},(\dots,v_{n})):b_{2}\times(\dots\times b_{n})\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(230)

\displaystyle=(\alpha^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v_{1}:b_{1},v^{\prime}:b_{2}\times(\dots\times b_{n})\vdash\phi[\dots]\rrbracket

(231)

\displaystyle=(\alpha^{-1})^{*}\alpha^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:b_{1}\times(\dots\times b_{n})\vdash\phi[\dots][\pi_{1}\ v/v_{1},\pi_{2}\ v/v^{\prime}]\rrbracket

(232)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,(v_{1},(\dots,v_{n})):b_{1}\times(\dots\times b_{n})\vdash\phi\rrbracket

by Lemma
C.32
.
∎

Corollary C.34.

R-BasicSimp
is sound.

Proof.

By Lemma
C.30
, it suffices to prove

(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{op})):\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times(\dots\times\sigma_{n})\mid\phi[\mathbf{op}(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})/v]\}\rrbracket\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{v:\tau\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

By Lemma
C.33
,

(233)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\mathbf{Prop}^{n}\mid\phi[\mathbf{op}(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})/v]\}\rrbracket

(234)

\displaystyle=\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,(v_{1},(\dots,v_{n})):\sigma_{1}\times(\dots\times\sigma_{n})\vdash\phi[\mathbf{op}(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})/v]\rrbracket

(235)

\displaystyle=\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,u:\sigma_{1}\times(\dots\times\sigma_{n})\vdash\phi[\mathbf{op}(u)/v]\rrbracket

(236)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid\phi\}\rrbracket

(237)

\displaystyle=\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket

By Lemma
C.3
,
C.7
,

(238)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,u:\sigma_{1}\times(\dots\times\sigma_{n})\vdash\phi[\mathbf{op}(u)/v]\rrbracket

(239)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{id},a(\mathbf{op})\mathrel{\circ}\pi_{2}\rangle^{*}(\pi_{1}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket

(240)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{op}))^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:\tau\vdash\phi\rrbracket

∎

Corollary C.35.

R-BasicBool
is sound

Proof.

By Lemma
C.30
, it suffices to prove

(241)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{op}))

\displaystyle:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\mid\psi_{t}\land\phi_{t}[()/v]\lor\psi_{f}\land\phi_{f}[()/v]\}\rrbracket

(242)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{v:1\mid\phi_{t}\}+\{v:1\mid\phi_{f}\}\rrbracket

By definition,

(243)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{v:1\mid\phi_{t}\}\rrbracket

(244)

\displaystyle=\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:1\vdash\phi_{t}\rrbracket

(245)

\displaystyle=\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\pi^{*}\langle\mathrm{id},{!}\rangle^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,v:1\vdash\phi_{t}\rrbracket

(246)

\displaystyle=\pi^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{t}[()/v]\rrbracket)

(247)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{v:1\mid\phi_{t}\}+\{v:1\mid\phi_{f}\}\rrbracket

(248)

\displaystyle=\{\langle\gamma,\iota_{1}\rangle\mid\gamma\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\cap\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{t}[()/v]\rrbracket\}\cup\{\langle\gamma,\iota_{2}\rangle\mid\gamma\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\cap\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{f}[()/v]\rrbracket\}

(249)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{t}[()/v]\rrbracket)\mathrel{\dot{\times}}\{\iota_{1}\}\lor(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{f}[()/v]\rrbracket)\mathrel{\dot{\times}}\{\iota_{2}\}

(250)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{op}))^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{v:1\mid\phi_{t}\}+\{v:1\mid\phi_{f}\}\rrbracket

(251)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{t}[()/v]\rrbracket)\mathrel{\dot{\times}}(a(\mathbf{op}))^{*}\{\iota_{1}\}

(252)

\displaystyle\qquad\lor(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{f}[()/v]\rrbracket)\mathrel{\dot{\times}}(a(\mathbf{op}))^{*}\{\iota_{2}\}

(253)

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{t}[()/v]\rrbracket)\land({!}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\pi_{2}^{*}(a(\mathbf{op}))^{*}\{\iota_{1}\}

(254)

\displaystyle\qquad\lor\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{f}[()/v]\rrbracket)\land({!}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\pi_{2}^{*}(a(\mathbf{op}))^{*}\{\iota_{2}\}

(255)

\displaystyle\geq\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{t}[()/v]\rrbracket\land({!}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\vdash\psi_{t}\rrbracket

(256)

\displaystyle\qquad\lor\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\vdash\phi_{f}[()/v]\rrbracket\land({!}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\vdash\psi_{f}\rrbracket)

(257)

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|,(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\vdash\phi_{t}[()/v]\land\psi_{t}\lor\phi_{f}[()/v]\land\psi_{f}\rrbracket

(258)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{(v_{1},\dots,v_{n}):\sigma_{1}\times\dots\times\sigma_{n}\mid\psi_{t}\land\phi_{t}[()/v]\lor\psi_{f}\land\phi_{f}[()/v]\}\rrbracket

∎

Lemma C.36.

R-Unif
is sound in the
\lambda_{\mathrm{HFL}}
-model for expected costs and weakest pre-expectations.

Proof.

By Lemma
C.30
, it suffices to prove

(259)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{unif}))

(260)

\displaystyle:\mathcal{A}^{\xi,\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:(x:\{x:\mathbf{real}\mid 0\leq x\land x\leq 1\})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid v\leq N\ x\}\rrbracket

(261)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathrel{\dot{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\xi,\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid v\leq\mathbf{unif}(N)\}\rrbracket

By definition,

(262)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\xi,\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:(x:\{x:\mathbf{real}\mid 0\leq x\land x\leq 1\})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid v\leq N\ x\}\rrbracket

(263)

\displaystyle=\{\prod_{\mathcal{A}^{\xi,\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\vdash\{x:\mathbf{real}\mid 0\leq x\land x\leq 1\}\rrbracket}\mathcal{A}^{\xi,\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},x:\{x:\mathbf{real}\mid 0\leq x\land x\leq 1\}\vdash\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid v\leq N\ x\}\rrbracket\}

(264)

\displaystyle=\{\langle\gamma,f\rangle:1\to\mathcal{A}^{\xi}\llbracket|\dot{\Gamma}|\rrbracket\times(\mathbb{R}\Rightarrow\Omega)\mid\gamma\in\mathcal{A}^{\xi,\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land

(265)

\displaystyle\qquad\forall x:1\to\mathbb{R},0\leq x\leq 1\implies\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\langle f,x\rangle\leq\mathbf{ev}\mathrel{\circ}\langle\mathcal{A}^{\xi}\llbracket N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\gamma,x\rangle\}

Recall that
a(\mathbf{unif}):\mathbb{R}\Rightarrow\Omega\to\Omega
is defined by
a(\mathbf{unif})(f)=\int_{0}^{1}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x
.
By monotonicity of
a(\mathbf{unif})
, if

\langle\gamma,f\rangle\in\mathcal{A}^{\xi,\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma},v:(x:\{x:\mathbf{real}\mid 0\leq x\land x\leq 1\})\to\{v:\mathbf{Prop}\mid v\leq N\ x\}\rrbracket

then

(\mathrm{id}\times a(\mathbf{unif}))\mathrel{\circ}\langle\gamma,f\rangle\in\{\langle\gamma,y\rangle\mid\gamma\in\mathcal{A}^{\xi,\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\dot{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land y\leq a(\mathbf{unif})\mathrel{\circ}\mathcal{A}^{\xi}\llbracket N\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\gamma\}

∎

C.5.
Proofs for Rules for Admissibility

Lemma C.37.

If
\llbracket a\rrbracket
is chain-closed, then
\llbracket a(M)\rrbracket
is chain-closed at any variable.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

Lemma C.38.

\llbracket\leq_{\mathbf{Prop}}\rrbracket
is chain-closed.

Proof.

Easy.
∎

Lemma C.39.

If
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in\mathbb{P}_{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket}
are admissible at
x_{i}
, then so are
Q_{1}\cap Q_{2}
and
Q_{1}\cup Q_{2}
.

Proof.

The only non-trivial part of the proof is the chain-closed-ness of
Q_{1}\cup Q_{2}
.
Assume
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f_{k}]\in Q_{1}\cup Q_{2}
holds for each
k
where
f_{0}\leq f_{1}\leq\dots
is an
\omega
-chain in
\mathbb{C}(1,\llbracket\sigma_{i}\rrbracket)
and
\gamma:1\to\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
Then either
\{k\mid\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f_{k}]\in Q_{1}\}
or
\{k\mid\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f_{k}]\in Q_{2}\}
must be an infinite set.
That is, we have an infinite sequence of indices
k_{0}\leq k_{1}\leq\dots
such that either
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{l}f_{k_{l}}]\in Q_{1}
or
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{l}f_{k_{l}}]\in Q_{2}
holds.
Since we have
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{l}f_{k_{l}}]=\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{k}f_{k}]
, we have
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{k}f_{k}]\in Q_{1}\cup Q_{2}
.
∎

Lemma C.40.

If
Q\in\mathbb{P}_{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket}
is admissible at
x_{i}
and
R\in\mathbb{P}_{\llbracket\Gamma\setminus x_{i}\rrbracket}
, then

(266)

\{\gamma:1\to\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\mid\gamma\in\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma;x_{i}}^{*}R\implies\gamma\in Q\}

is admissible at
x_{i}
.
Here
\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma;v}:\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\to\llbracket\Gamma\setminus v\rrbracket
is the canonical projection.

\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma,v:\sigma;v}=\pi_{1}\qquad\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma,x:\sigma;v}=\mathrm{proj}_{\Gamma;v}\times\mathrm{id}

Proof.

Note that for any
\gamma:1\to\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
and
f:1\to\llbracket\sigma_{i}\rrbracket
, if
\gamma\in\mathrm{proj}^{*}R
, then
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f]\in\mathrm{proj}^{*}R
.
For and
\gamma:1\to\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
and any
\omega
-chain
f_{0}\leq f_{1}\leq\dots
in
\mathbb{C}(1,\llbracket\sigma_{i}\rrbracket)
, if
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f_{j}]
is in (
266
) for any
j
, then there are two cases.

•

If
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f_{j}]\notin\mathrm{proj}^{*}R
for some
j
, then
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{j}f_{j}]\notin\mathrm{proj}^{*}R
.

•

If
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f_{j}]\in\mathrm{proj}^{*}R
for any
j
, then
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto f_{j}]\in Q
for any
j
.
Therefore,
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{j}f_{j}]\in Q
.

In any case,
\gamma[x_{i}\mapsto\sup_{j}f_{j}]
is in (
266
).
∎

Lemma C.41.

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathrm{adm}(v,\phi)
, then
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\phi\rrbracket
is admissible at
v
.

Proof.

By induction on derivation of
\Gamma\vdash\mathrm{adm}(v,\phi)
.

•

Adm-Leq
:
\llbracket\mathbf{Prop}\rrbracket
has a bottom element because it is an EM
T
-algebra.
For each
\gamma:1\to\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
, we have
\gamma[v\mapsto\bot_{\llbracket\mathbf{Prop}\rrbracket}]\in\llbracket v\leq_{\mathbf{Prop}}M\rrbracket
because
\bot_{\llbracket\mathbf{Prop}\rrbracket}\leq\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\circ}\gamma[v\mapsto\bot_{\llbracket\mathbf{Prop}\rrbracket}]
.

\llbracket v\leq_{\mathbf{Prop}}M\rrbracket
is chain-closed at
v
by Lemma
C.37
,
C.38
.

•

Adm-And
,
Adm-Or
: by Lemma
C.39
.

•

Adm-Imp
: by Lemma
C.40

∎
